# 2026 FIFA World Cup

Several terms redirect here. For other uses, see [2026 World Cup (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_World_Cup_\(disambiguation\)).

The **2026 FIFA World Cup**[^cite_note-3] was the 23rd [FIFA World Cup](11370.md), the [quadrennial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrennial) international men's [soccer](10568.md) championship contested by [the national teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_men's_national_association_football_teams) of the member associations of [FIFA](11049.md). The tournament began on June 11, 2026, and concluded on July 19 with [Spain](609825.md) winning the championship for the second time.[^cite_note-Schedule-4] It was jointly hosted by 16 cities—11 in the United States, 3 in Mexico, and 2 in Canada. The tournament was the first FIFA World Cup to be hosted by three countries and the first to include 48 teams, an expansion from the previous 32-team format.[^cite_note-5]

The 2026 tournament was the second World Cup to be hosted by multiple nations, after the [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup) edition. Mexico became the first country to host the World Cup three times, having hosted the [1970](157227.md) and [1986](157231.md) tournaments.[^cite_note-qt-6] The United States previously hosted the [World Cup in 1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup), while it was Canada's first time hosting the tournament.[^cite_note-qt-6] The event returned to its traditional June–July schedule after the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md) in Qatar, which was uniquely held in November and December to avoid [Qatar's extreme summer heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Qatar).[^cite_note-7] [Canada](723688.md), [Mexico](808402.md), and the [United States](582488.md) qualified automatically as hosts, while [Cape Verde](1205575.md), [Curaçao](24862626.md), [Jordan](1090065.md), and [Uzbekistan](1032413.md) made their World Cup debuts.[^cite_note-qt-6]

For the first time in the tournament's history, the top four teams from the [FIFA Men's World Ranking](719095.md) at the time of the contest ([Argentina](454699.md), [Spain](609825.md), [France](250197.md), and [England](9904.md)) advanced to the semifinals.[^cite_note-8] Spain won [the final](75845879.md) against defending champion Argentina 1–0 after [extra time](633242.md), winning its second title and first since [2010](168079.md). Spain also made history by being the first nation to become the reigning champion for both the men's and [women](45271353.md)'s World Cups,[^cite_note-9] and the first team to win the World Cup while conceding only one goal during the tournament. France captain [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) won his second consecutive [Golden Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Boot) for scoring the most goals (10) during the tournament. He became the [all-time leading goalscorer](43255516.md) at the World Cup and the first player to win multiple World Cup Golden Boots.[^cite_note-10] The average number of goals per match in the tournament was 2.96, the highest since the 1970 edition.[^cite_note-11]

Spain captain [Rodri](48849865.md) won the [Golden Ball](3482503.md) as the best player of the tournament. [Unai Simón](58209770.md) won the [Golden Glove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Glove) as the tournament's best [goalkeeper](5348091.md), while [Pau Cubarsí](74669990.md) won the [Young Player Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Young_Player_Award) as the tournament's best young player. Nearly 7 million people attended matches during the tournament, surpassing the 1994 edition to set a new record for the [highest attendance in World Cup history](3602475.md#Attendance).[^cite_note-USATodayRecord2026-12] The 2026 World Cup reportedly generated a record \$15 billion in revenue.[^cite_note-13]

## Format

### Expansion

The idea of expanding the tournament had been suggested as early as 2013 by then-[UEFA](32332.md) president [Michel Platini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Platini),[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-15] as well as in 2016 by current FIFA president [Gianni Infantino](9286117.md).[^cite_note-16] Opponents of the proposal argued that the number of matches played was already at an unacceptable level, that the expansion would dilute the quality of the matches,[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] and that the decision was driven by political rather than sporting concerns, specifically that Infantino was trying to [win his election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_FIFA_Extraordinary_Congress) by promising to bring more countries to the World Cup.[^cite_note-19]

Starting with the 2026 edition, the World Cup expanded to 48 teams, an increase of 16 teams compared to the previous seven tournaments.[^cite_note-48teams-20] As approved by the [FIFA Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Council) on March 14, 2023, the teams were split into 12 groups of 4 teams, with the top 2 teams in each group and the 8 best third-place teams progressing to a new round of 32.[^cite_note-IMC-21] It was the first expansion and format change since [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_1998).[^cite_note-22]

The total number of matches played increased from 64 to 104, and the number of matches played by teams reaching the semifinals increased from 7 to 8. The tournament lasted 39 days, an increase from the 32 days of the [2014](656933.md) and [2018](13327177.md) tournaments, and from the 29 days of the [2022](17742072.md) tournament,[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] although each team still played three group matches.[^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] The final matchday at club level for players named in the final squads was May 24, 2026; clubs had to release their players by May 25, with exceptions granted to players participating in continental club competition finals up until May 30. The combined rest, release, and tournament periods remained 56 days, the same as in the [2010](168079.md), 2014, and 2018 World Cups.[^cite_note-IMC-21]

#### Other expansion formats explored

After the expansion to 48 teams was approved on January 10, 2017, it was initially decided that the tournament would include 16 groups of 3 teams, with the top two teams of each group progressing to a round of 32.[^cite_note-48teams-20][^cite_note-27] This format would have included 80 matches in total, with the maximum number of matches per team remaining at seven, although each team would have played one fewer group match than in the 2022 tournament. The tournament would still have been completed within 32 days.[^cite_note-28] This format was chosen over three alternatives involving 40 to 48 teams, 76 to 88 matches, and a minimum of 1 to 4 matches per team.[^cite_note-32]

Critics of this format argued that the use of three-team groups with two teams progressing significantly increased the risk of collusion between teams.[^cite_note-guyon-33] This prompted FIFA to suggest that [penalty shootouts](511302.md) could be used to prevent draws in the group stage,[^cite_note-34] although even then some risk of collusion would remain, as it was possible for the two teams playing the last group match to engineer a [circle of death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_of_Death_\(sporting\)) result that would allow both of them to qualify.[^cite_note-guyon-33] The tournament format was finalized in 2023, with an announcement that there would be 12 groups of 4 teams.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36]

### New rules

The 2026 FIFA World Cup introduced several [rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_of_the_Game_\(association_football\)) changes, primarily designed to reduce [time-wasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running_out_the_clock).[^cite_note-newrules-37] The new rules included:

- 10-second substitutions: Players being [substituted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substitute_\(association_football\)) had ten seconds to exit the field; otherwise, the substitute had to wait for one minute before entering the match.

- 5-second restarts: A visual 5-second countdown was shown by the referee for throw-ins and goal kicks in situations of time-wasting. If the ball was not put into play in time, a restart was awarded to the opposing team; a [corner kick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corner_kick) was awarded if the infraction occurred with a goal kick.

- Medical treatment: Any outfield player who received medical attention on the field had to leave the field and wait for 1 minute before returning to play.

- Expanded [video assistant referee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_assistant_referee) (VAR): The VAR could review and overturn wrongly awarded corner kicks, certain attacking fouls, and clear mistakes on red cards given from a second yellow card.

- Mouth-covering red cards: To stop confrontational or insulting behavior which was concealed to avoid [lip-reading](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lip-reading), any player who covered his mouth with his hand, arm, or shirt while confronting an opponent was [sent off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_card_\(soccer\)).

- Player leaving the field in protest: Players or officials who left the field in protest were sent off.

  [^cite_note-newrules-37]

Another change was the tiebreaker formula used when two or more teams finish the group stage with an equal number of points. The first tiebreaker criteria was the number of points earned in head-to-head matches between the tied teams, not the [goal difference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goal_difference) among all matches.[^cite_note-41]

### Mandatory breaks

Cooling or [hydration breaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydration_break) were introduced at the [2014 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_World_Cup) in Brazil. For the 2026 World Cup, FIFA introduced mandatory three-minute hydration [breaks in every half for all matches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time-out_\(sport\)). Broadcasters were permitted to run [commercials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_timeout) during these pauses.[^cite_note-Breaks-42] Since the clock did not stop during the breaks, the three minutes were automatically included in the [stoppage time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoppage_time) for each half.[^cite_note-Yahoo-43]

### Match schedule

The match schedule, without group assignments, was announced on February 4, 2024.[^cite_note-46] On June 13, FIFA released an updated schedule, with specific pairings assigned to venues for the knockout stage.[^cite_note-47] The full schedule was unveiled in a live broadcast on December 6, 2025, the day after the [draw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_draw).[^cite_note-48] The opening match of the tournament included Mexico, and took place on June 11, 2026, at the [Estadio Azteca](1834902.md) in [Mexico City](18987.md).[^cite_note-49] Canada's first match occurred on June 12 at [BMO Field](3163911.md) in [Toronto](64646.md), while the United States' opening match took place on the same day at [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in [Inglewood](107640.md). The national team of each host nation played its three group stage matches in its own country.[^cite_note-ny-44]

[AT\&T Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT\&T_Stadium) in [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington,_Texas) hosted the most matches of any venue, with nine. The U.S. hosted 78 matches, including all quarterfinals, semifinals, and the [final](75845879.md), which was played at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford](136878.md) on July 19. Canada and Mexico each hosted 13 matches. Each tournament venue, except for the [Estadio Akron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estadio_Akron), hosted at least one knockout stage match.[^cite_note-50] The match schedule overlapped with the [2026 Canadian Football League season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_CFL_season), resulting in scheduling conflicts and loss of home games for the [Toronto Argonauts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_Argonauts) and [BC Lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BC_Lions).[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52] The match schedule also affected the [2026 Major League Baseball season](80882200.md) schedules of the [Kansas City Royals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City_Royals), [Philadelphia Phillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Phillies), [Seattle Mariners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Mariners), and [Texas Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Rangers_\(baseball\)), with home stadiums near World Cup venues.[^cite_note-\:0-53]

Host cities were geographically grouped into three regions:[^cite_note-Schedule-4]

- Western Region (Vancouver, Seattle, San Francisco Bay Area, Los Angeles)

- Central Region (Guadalajara, Mexico City, Monterrey, Houston, Dallas, Kansas City)

- Eastern Region (Atlanta, Miami, Toronto, Boston, Philadelphia, New York/[New Jersey](21648.md))

However, even during the group stage, about half of the teams had to play matches in two different regions.[^cite_note-Schedule-4]

## Host selection

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup bids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_bids)

The [United 2026 bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_2026_FIFA_World_Cup_bid) beat a [rival bid by Morocco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morocco_2026_FIFA_World_Cup_bid) during a final vote at the [68th FIFA Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/68th_FIFA_Congress) in [Moscow](19004.md) in 2018. The previous year, FIFA president Gianni Infantino had stated that Europe ([UEFA](32332.md) confederation) and Asia ([AFC](715249.md)) would be excluded from the bidding following the selection of [Russia](13327177.md) for the 2018 World Cup and [Qatar](17742072.md) for the 2022 tournament.[^cite_note-guardian-54] Therefore, the 2026 World Cup could be hosted by one of the remaining four confederations: [CONCACAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONCACAF) (North America; last hosted in [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup)), [CAF](715265.md) (Africa; last hosted in [2010](168079.md)), [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONMEBOL) (South America; last hosted in [2014](656933.md)), or [OFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceania_Football_Confederation) (Oceania, never hosted), or potentially by AFC or UEFA in case no bid from the others met FIFA's requirements.[^cite_note-55]

Co-hosting of the World Cup—which had been suspended by FIFA after the [2002 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup)—was approved for the enlarged 2026 tournament.[^cite_note-fifacouncil-56] Canada, Mexico, and the United States had each considered bidding for the tournament separately; the United joint bid was announced on April 10, 2017.[^cite_note-\:1-57][^cite_note-ol-58] The Moroccan bid was announced that August.[^cite_note-59]

### Voting

Voting for the host was open to 203 FIFA member associations.[^cite_note-60] The United bid won with 134 votes, while the Morocco bid received 65 votes.[^cite_note-ol-58][^cite_note-61] Iran voted for neither of the two bids, while Cuba, Slovenia, and Spain abstained from voting. [Ghana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghana_Football_Association) had been suspended by FIFA for corruption and was therefore ineligible to vote.[^cite_note-67] Morocco was later selected as a co-host of the [2030 FIFA World Cup](39367087.md), alongside Portugal, Spain, Argentina, Paraguay, and Uruguay.

Location map of the 2026 FIFA World Cup venues

During the bidding process, 41 cities with 42 existing, fully functional venues with regular tenants (except [Olympic Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Stadium_\(Montreal\)) in [Montreal](7954681.md)) and two venues then under construction ([Allegiant Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegiant_Stadium) in [Las Vegas](47737.md) and [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in [Los Angeles](18110.md)) submitted to be part of the bid: three venues in three cities in Mexico, six venues in six cities in Canada, and 35 venues in 32 cities in the United States.[^cite_note-bids-68] A first-round elimination removed nine venues and nine cities. A second-round elimination cut an additional nine venues in six cities, while three venues in three cities ([Soldier Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier_Field) in [Chicago](6886.md), [U.S. Bank Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Bank_Stadium) in [Minneapolis](6097240.md), and [BC Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BC_Place) in [Vancouver](32706.md)) dropped out because FIFA was unwilling to negotiate financial details.[^cite_note-69] After Montreal dropped out in July 2021 because it lacked provincial funding and support to renovate Olympic Stadium,[^cite_note-70] Vancouver rejoined the bid as a candidate city in April 2022,[^cite_note-vancouver_candidatecity-71] bringing the total number to 24 venues, each in its own city or metropolitan area.[^cite_note-72]

On June 16, 2022, the sixteen host cities–two in Canada, three in Mexico, and eleven in the United States–were announced by FIFA: Atlanta, Boston, Dallas, Guadalajara, Houston, Kansas City, Los Angeles, Mexico City, Miami, Monterrey, New York/New Jersey, Philadelphia, San Francisco Bay Area, Seattle, Toronto, and Vancouver.[^cite_note-73] Eight of the sixteen chosen stadiums had permanent [artificial turf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_turf) surfaces that were replaced with grass under the direction of FIFA and a [University of Tennessee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tennessee)–[Michigan State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_State_University) research team. Depending on the venue's climate, the turf used was either a hybrid of 84% [Kentucky bluegrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poa_pratensis) and 16% [perennial ryegrass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lolium_perenne) (for cooler temperatures), or [Bermuda grass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cynodon_dactylon) (for warmer temperatures).[^cite_note-utk-fifa-74][^cite_note-tsn-henry-26-75][^cite_note-conv-tr-g-s-b-26-76]

Four venues (Atlanta, Dallas, Houston, and Vancouver) were indoor stadiums that used [retractable roof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retractable_roof) systems, all equipped with [climate control](57169.md), while a fifth, [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in Los Angeles, was open-air but had a translucent roof and no climate control.[^cite_note-77] The host of the [final match](75845879.md)—[MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford, New Jersey](136878.md)—was announced by FIFA on February 4, 2024.[^cite_note-ESPN-Schedule-78]

Although there were [soccer-specific stadiums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soccer-specific_stadium) in Canada and the United States, the largest soccer-specific stadium in the U.S., [Geodis Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geodis_Park) in [Nashville, Tennessee](22018.md), seated 30,000, fell short of FIFA's minimum requirement of 40,000 seats (Toronto's [BMO Field](3163911.md) was expanded from 30,000 to 45,500 for this tournament).[^cite_note-79] Stadiums including [Mercedes-Benz Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_Stadium) in [Atlanta](3138.md); [Gillette Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillette_Stadium) in [Foxborough, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxborough,_Massachusetts); and [Lumen Field](206812.md) in [Seattle](11388236.md) have been used by both [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL) and [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS) teams.[^cite_note-80] Although the Canadian and American stadiums were primarily used for [gridiron football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gridiron_football), they were also designed to accommodate soccer matches.[^cite_note-81]

[Mexico City](18987.md) was the only capital of the three host nations chosen as a venue site; [Ottawa](22219.md) and [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), joined [Bonn](3295.md) ([West Germany, 1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup)) and [Tokyo](30057.md) ([Japan, 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup)) as the only capital cities not selected to host World Cup matches. Washington was a host city candidate, but the poor state of [Northwest Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Stadium) caused the city to combine its bid with nearby [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore)'s [M\&T Bank Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M\&T_Bank_Stadium), which was unsuccessful. Other cities eliminated from the final hosting list were [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati), [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver), [Nashville](22018.md), [Orlando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando,_Florida), and [Edmonton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmonton). Ottawa's candidate venue, [TD Place Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TD_Place_Stadium), was eliminated early on for insufficient capacity.[^cite_note-82] Eight of the metropolitan areas involved had previously hosted World Cup matches ([Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles), [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area), [New York/New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area), and [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Boston) in [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup); [Guadalajara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadalajara_metropolitan_area) and [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Mexico_City) in both [1970](157227.md) and [1986](157231.md); [Monterrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monterrey_metropolitan_area) in 1986), but none of the stadiums used in the 1994 FIFA World Cup were used in this tournament (though Gillette Stadium and MetLife Stadium were located at the same sites as two of the 1994 venues, [Foxboro Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxboro_Stadium) and [Giants Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giants_Stadium) respectively).[^cite_note-83] Soldier Field in Chicago, the [Cotton Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton_Bowl_\(stadium\)) in Dallas, and the [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_\(stadium\)) in [Pasadena](92408.md) (Los Angeles area) were the only stadiums in the bidding process to have hosted matches in 1994, but none of them were selected.[^cite_note-bids-68] [Estadio Azteca](1834902.md) in Mexico City was the only stadium in this tournament that had previously been used for a World Cup, in both 1970 and 1986; it thus became the only stadium to be used for three World Cups.

## Teams

### Qualification

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup qualification](49459509.md)



&#x20; Team qualified

&#x20; Team eliminated

&#x20; Team withdrew or suspended

&#x20; Not a FIFA member

On August 31, 2022, FIFA president Gianni Infantino confirmed that six CONCACAF teams would qualify for the World Cup, with Canada, Mexico, and the United States automatically qualifying as hosts.[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] This was confirmed by the [FIFA Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Council) on February 14, 2023.[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91]

Immediately prior to the 67th [FIFA Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Congress), the FIFA Council approved the slot allocation during a meeting in [Manama](20481.md), Bahrain.[^cite_note-slot-92][^cite_note-93] This included an [inter-confederation playoff tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(inter-confederation_play-offs\)) involving six teams to decide the last two FIFA World Cup spots.[^cite_note-fifa_council-94] The six teams in the playoffs comprised one team from each confederation excluding [UEFA](32332.md), and one additional team from the confederation of the host countries ([CONCACAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONCACAF)). Two of those teams were seeded based on the [World Rankings](719095.md), and they played the winners of two knockout matches between the four unseeded teams for the two FIFA World Cup berths. The four-match tournament was played in Mexico, one of the host countries, and was also used as a test event for the FIFA World Cup.[^cite_note-slot-92]

The ratification of slot allocation also gave the [OFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceania_Football_Confederation) a guaranteed berth in the final tournament for the first time: the 2026 FIFA World Cup was the first tournament in which all six confederations had at least one guaranteed berth and also the first time since the [2010 edition](168079.md) in which all confederations had a team qualify for the World Cup finals.[^cite_note-slot-92]

Of the 48 qualified teams, 26 also appeared in the [2022 edition](17742072.md). Highlights include:

- [Cape Verde](1205575.md),

  [^cite_note-95]

  &#x20;[Curaçao](24862626.md),

  [^cite_note-jq-96]

  &#x20;[Jordan](1090065.md), and [Uzbekistan](1032413.md)

  [^cite_note-97]

  [^cite_note-98]

  &#x20;made their World Cup debuts.

- [Qatar](1111350.md) qualified for the tournament through qualification for the first time, as its only previous appearance was as host in 2022.

  [^cite_note-99]

- [DR Congo](1030243.md)

  [^cite_note-100]

  &#x20;and [Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiti_national_football_team) returned to the tournament after appearing in their only previous tournament in [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup).

  [^cite_note-jq-96]

  [^cite_note-101]

- [Iraq](1039652.md) returned to the tournament after appearing in its only previous tournament in [1986](157231.md).

  [^cite_note-102]

- [Austria](695226.md), [Norway](203277.md),

  [^cite_note-103]

  &#x20;and [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team) returned to the tournament after last appearing in [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup).

  [^cite_note-104]

Four-time champion [Italy](362466.md) failed to qualify after losing the [European playoff final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_second_round#Path_A) to Bosnia and Herzegovina on [penalties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penalty_shootout), becoming the first former champion to miss three consecutive World Cups;[^cite_note-105] as in [2018](13327177.md) and 2022, Italy was the only former champion that did not qualify. With a [FIFA Men's World Ranking](719095.md) of 12, Italy was also the highest-ranking team that did not qualify.[^cite_note-106]

### Draw

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup draw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_draw)



From left to right, FIFA president [Gianni Infantino](9286117.md), U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md), Mexican president [Claudia Sheinbaum](57813311.md), and Canadian prime minister [Mark Carney](13571049.md) at the start of the draw.

The draw took place on December 5, 2025, at the [Kennedy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Center) in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-116] The draw ceremony began with FIFA presenting the first [FIFA Peace Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_draw) to [Donald Trump](4848272.md),[^cite_note-Guardian-main-117] an award which fueled scrutiny and ridicule among human rights groups, analysts, and others.[^cite_note-AJ-Analysis-118][^cite_note-Amnesty-119][^cite_note-NewHumanitarian-120][^cite_note-121]

The 48 teams were divided into four pots of 12. Pot 1 consisted of the three hosts and the top nine teams from the November 2025 [FIFA Men's World Ranking](719095.md). Pots 2, 3, and 4 consisted of the remaining teams according to the ranking. The four winners of the [UEFA playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_second_round) and the two winners of the inter-confederation playoffs were not known at the time of the draw and thus were automatically allocated to Pot 4. The 12 groups were randomly formed by selecting one team from each of the four pots. With the exception of UEFA, no group could have more than one team from the same confederation drawn into it.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123]

The three host nations were pre-allocated to three groups for scheduling purposes. Mexico was placed in Group A, Canada in Group B, and the United States in Group D.[^cite_note-Schedule-4][^cite_note-124]

The confederation restriction applied to all three potential winners of the inter-confederation playoffs. FIFA also announced that, "in the interest of ensuring competitive balance", the teams ranked first (Spain), second (Argentina), third (France), and fourth (England) would be drawn into opposite pathways. Therefore, should these teams win their groups, they would be unable to meet until the semifinals, and the first and second ranked teams would be unable to meet until the final.[^cite_note-125] The draw started with Pot 1 and ended with Pot 4, with each team selected and then allocated into the first available group alphabetically. For the purpose of the match schedule, the Pot 1 teams were automatically drawn into position 1 of each group. For the remaining pots, FIFA established a predetermined pattern to define the position of teams based on their pot and the group they were drawn into.

### Team base camps

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup base camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_base_camps)

Each of the 48 teams established a "base camp", where they stayed and trained before and during the World Cup.[^cite_note-135]

### Squads

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup squads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_squads)

Before submitting their final squad for the World Cup, teams named a provisional squad of between 35 and 55 players one month prior to the tournament. Teams were required to name their final squads by June 2. If a player became too injured or ill to participate in the tournament, he could be replaced by another player from the provisional squad until 24 hours before the team's first match. However, an injured or ill goalkeeper could be replaced by another goalkeeper from the provisional squad at any time during the tournament.[^cite_note-136]

### Travel distances

Teams participating in the 2026 World Cup had to travel an average of 5,146 total miles (8,281.7 km) between venues during the tournament. For the [2014 tournament](656933.md) in Brazil, the average distance was 7,054 miles (11,352.2 km), while teams playing in the [2018 tournament](13327177.md) in Russia traveled an average of 5,441 miles (8,756.4 km).[^cite_note-Distance-137]

## Officiating

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_officials)

On April 9, 2026, FIFA announced 52 referees, 88 assistant referees, and 30 [video assistant referees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_assistant_referee) for the tournament.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] The 2026 World Cup featured six female match officials, equalling the record for the highest number of female officials appointed to a men's World Cup.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] [Tori Penso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tori_Penso), [Brooke Mayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_Mayo), and [Kathryn Nesbitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathryn_Nesbitt) became the second all-female on-field refereeing trio at a men's World Cup, officiating the Group A [match between Czech Republic and South Africa](81660180.md#Czech_Republic_vs_South_Africa) at Atlanta Stadium.[^cite_note-142]

On June 9, Somali referee [Omar Artan](80994536.md) was refused entry into the United States despite holding valid travel documents, and FIFA later removed him from the list of referees.[^cite_note-143] FIFA stated that Artan would receive his full World Cup salary despite not being able to officiate in the tournament.[^cite_note-144]

## Ceremonies

### Opening ceremonies

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup opening ceremonies](83423432.md)



Inside [Estadio Azteca](1834902.md) during the Mexican opening ceremony prior to the first match of the 2026 FIFA World Cup.

The tournament featured three opening ceremonies, one for each of the hosts.[^cite_note-145] The opening ceremony in Mexico took place on June 11, 2026, at Estadio Azteca.[^cite_note-146] Mexican singer [Alejandro Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Fern%C3%A1ndez) performed "[Himno Nacional Mexicano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himno_Nacional_Mexicano)" and South African singer [Tyla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyla) performed the "[National Anthem of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Anthem_of_South_Africa)".[^cite_note-147] On June 12, the Canadian opening ceremony took place at BMO Field in Toronto. Canadian-American musician [Alanis Morissette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alanis_Morissette) performed "[O Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O_Canada)" while Serbian-Canadian violinist Aleksandar Gajić performed "[Državna himna Bosne i Hercegovine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_anthem_of_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina)".[^cite_note-148] On the same day, the opening ceremony in the United States took place at SoFi Stadium in Los Angeles. American country duo [Dan + Shay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_+_Shay) performed "[The Star-Spangled Banner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Star-Spangled_Banner)" while Paraguayan soul duo Purahei Soul performed "[Himno Nacional Paraguayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraguayan_National_Anthem)".[^cite_note-149]

### 1,000th FIFA World Cup match

The [Group F](81660196.md#Tunisia_vs_Japan) game between Tunisia and Japan, played at [Estadio BBVA](17130610.md) in [Monterrey](40455.md) on June 20, was the 1,000th match in [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) history. To mark the occasion, [FIFA](11049.md) organized a special pre-match ceremony. [Princess Takamado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisako,_Princess_Takamado), the Honorary Patron of the [Japan Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Football_Association), attended the event as a special guest.[^cite_note-150] [Japan](993546.md) won the match 4–0, securing its place in the knockout stage, while [Tunisia](1029953.md) suffered its second consecutive defeat and was eliminated from the tournament.[^cite_note-151]

### Independence Day ceremonies



[The Roots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roots) performing during the Philadelphia ceremony

On July 4, ceremonies took place at [Lincoln Financial Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Financial_Field) in Philadelphia (Paraguay vs France) and [NRG Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NRG_Stadium) in Houston (Canada vs Morocco) to honor the [United States Semiquincentennial](57473130.md).[^cite_note-152] The Houston ceremony featured pyrotechnics, video-board graphics, and a performance of "The Star-Spangled Banner" by [United States Navy Band](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Navy_Band) Chief Musician Maia Rodriguez. The Philadelphia ceremony featured pyrotechnics, video-board signage, and a performance of "The Star-Spangled Banner" by [Idina Menzel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idina_Menzel), as well as musical performances by [the Roots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roots), ceremonial elements featuring the [Philadelphia Boys Choir & Chorale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Boys_Choir_&_Chorale) and [Miss Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miss_Pennsylvania), Stephanie Skinner, a [card stunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Card_stunt), a flyby of VFA-11 and VFA-81 squadrons from [Naval Air Station Oceana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Air_Station_Oceana), and a halftime show from [DJ Jazzy Jeff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DJ_Jazzy_Jeff).[^cite_note-153]

### Closing ceremony

The closing ceremony took place before the [World Cup final](75845879.md) at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in New Jersey, and featured performances by [Laura Pausini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Pausini), [Nicole Scherzinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicole_Scherzinger), [Robbie Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robbie_Williams), [IShowSpeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IShowSpeed), and [Post Malone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post_Malone), as well as an appearance from [Tom Cruise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Cruise). [Jennifer Hudson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Hudson) performed "The Star-Spangled Banner".[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155]

### Final halftime show



The World Cup final halftime show at MetLife Stadium

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup final halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_final_halftime_show)

The final match featured the first-ever [Super Bowl](27718.md)–style [halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_final_halftime_show) in FIFA World Cup history. [Madonna](142056.md), [Shakira](6479315.md), and [BTS](39862325.md) headlined the 11-minute halftime show, which was curated by [Chris Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Martin) of [Coldplay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coldplay) and produced by [Global Citizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Citizen_\(organization\)).[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-BBC-Halftime-157] [Justin Bieber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Bieber), [Burna Boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burna_Boy), [Gustavo Dudamel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustavo_Dudamel), [the Muppets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Muppets), and the [PS22 Chorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PS22_Chorus) (featuring Coldplay) also performed in the show.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159]

## Group stage



Result of countries participating in the 2026 FIFA World Cup&#x20;

&#x20; Champion

&#x20; Runner-up

&#x20; Third place

&#x20; Fourth place

&#x20; Quarterfinals

&#x20; Round of 16

&#x20; Round of 32

&#x20; Group stage

The [round-robin](939742.md) group stage was played in twelve groups (A to L) of four teams each, from June 11 to June 27.[^cite_note-160] Teams were awarded three points for a win, one for a draw, and none for a loss. Following the conclusion of group play, the top two teams of each group, along with the [eight best third-place teams](#Ranking_of_third-place_teams), advanced to the [knockout stage](#Knockout_stage).

*All times are local.*

| The ranking of teams in each group was determined by the points obtained in all group matches. If two or more teams were equal on points, the following criteria were used to determine the ranking:[^cite_note-regulations-161] Most points obtained in the group matches played between the teams concerned;Superior goal difference in the group matches played between the teams concerned;Most goals scored in the group matches played between the teams concerned; If, after having applied criteria a to c, teams still had an equal ranking, criteria a to c were reapplied exclusively to the matches between the teams who were still level to determine their final rankings.[^cite_note-162] If this procedure did not lead to a decision, criteria d to h applied. Superior goal difference in all group matches;Most goals scored in all group matches;Highest team conduct ("fair play") score in all group matches (only one deduction could be applied to a player or team coach/official in a single match): Yellow card: −1 point;Indirect red card (second yellow card): −3 points;Direct red card: −4 points;Yellow card and direct red card: −5 points;Better position in the most recent [FIFA Men's World Ranking](719095.md);Better position in progressively older FIFA Men's World Rankings until teams could be separated. |
| --- |

Rules for classification: 1) Points; 2) Goal difference; 3) Goals scored; 4) Team conduct ("fair play") score; 5) Latest [FIFA ranking](719095.md) (June 11, 2026); 6) Previous FIFA ranking(s) report.\
Notes:

The specific matchups involving the third-place teams depended on which eight third-place teams qualified for the round of 32, but not on their relative rankings. The [495 possible combinations](81667005.md#Combinations_of_matches_in_the_round_of_32) were published in Annex C of the tournament regulations. Because of the specific combination of qualified teams, option number 67 of annex C was applicable.[^cite_note-regulations-161]

## Knockout stage

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup knockout stage](81667005.md)

The knockout stage used a [single-elimination format](939680.md), starting with the newly introduced round of 32 on June 28 and culminating with the [final](75845879.md) on July 19, with a [match for third place](#Match_for_third_place) the day before. If the score was even after regulation time in a match, 30 minutes of [extra time](633242.md#Association_football) were added. If still tied after this, a [penalty shootout](511302.md) determined the winner.[^cite_note-regulations-161]

### Discipline

A player or team official was automatically suspended for the next match of the tournament for the following offenses:[^cite_note-regulations-161]

- Receiving a red card (red card suspensions could be extended for serious offenses).

- Receiving two yellow cards in the tournament.

  [^cite_note-208]

  &#x20;This yellow card count was reset after the completion of the group stage and again after the quarterfinals.

  [^cite_note-FIFA_Council_April_28-209]

  &#x20;Resets did not apply to suspensions; a player whose yellow card count reached two in the match immediately before a reset was still suspended for the next match.

  [^cite_note-210]

During qualification for the World Cup, [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) was [sent off](3090014.md) for violent conduct in Portugal's penultimate match against the [Republic of Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Ireland_national_football_team), with such an offense typically resulting in a ban of at least two matches. Ronaldo was handed a three-match ban, though the final two matches of the ban were suspended for a one-year probationary period, making him eligible to appear in Portugal's opening World Cup match.[^cite_note-211] On May 8, 2026, the Bureau of the [FIFA Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Council) amended the tournament regulations so that pending one- or two-match suspensions resulting from red cards for two yellow cards, denying an obvious goal-scoring opportunity, or serious foul play during qualification would no longer be carried forward to the final competition.[^cite_note-212] This exempted Argentina's [Nicolás Otamendi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Otamendi), Ecuador's [Moisés Caicedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mois%C3%A9s_Caicedo), and Qatar's [Tarek Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarek_Salman) from serving their qualifying-round suspensions during the tournament, with the bans to instead be served in a subsequent competition.[^cite_note-213]

During the round of 32, United States player [Folarin Balogun](60360233.md) was sent off, and was expected to be banned for the forthcoming round of 16 game against [Belgium](679716.md). Following lobbying from U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md),[^cite_note-214] FIFA suspended the ban.[^cite_note-BalogunAthletic-215] The [Royal Belgian Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Belgian_Football_Association), Belgium manager [Rudi Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudi_Garcia), and UEFA were critical of the decision.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217]

Following the full-time whistle in the final, Argentina player [Leandro Paredes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leandro_Paredes) was involved in altercations with Spain players [Eric García](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Garc%C3%ADa_\(footballer,_born_2001\)) and [Gavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gavi_\(footballer\)). While FIFA initially reported a red card for these incidents, the organization later confirmed that Paredes was not punished at the time.[^cite_note-218]

### Prize money

In April 2026, FIFA confirmed the prizes for all participating nations. The total distribution of prize money for the expanded tournament was \$871 million, nearly double the amount from the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md). Of this total, \$671 million was allocated for performance-based prizes, with the remaining \$200 million paid out among teams regardless of performance. Each qualified team received preparation money in the amount of \$2.5 million; a team that was eliminated in the group stage would receive a minimum total amount \$12.5 million at the tournament, with the winner receiving a total of \$52.5 million.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]&#x20;

| Place | Teams | Amount (in millions) |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Per team | Total |  |  |
| Champion | 1 | \$50 | \$50 |
| Runner-up | 1 | \$33 | \$33 |
| Third place | 1 | \$29 | \$29 |
| Fourth place | 1 | \$27 | \$27 |
| 5th–8th place (quarterfinals) | 4 | \$19 | \$76 |
| 9th–16th place (round of 16) | 8 | \$15 | \$120 |
| 17th–32nd place (round of 32) | 16 | \$11 | \$176 |
| 33rd–48th place (group stage) | 16 | \$10 | \$160 |
| Total | 48 | \$671 |  |

## Awards

See also: [FIFA World Cup awards](3482503.md)



Left to right: [Young Player Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Young_Player_Award) winner [Pau Cubarsí](74669990.md), [Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Ball) winner [Rodri](48849865.md), and [Golden Glove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Glove) winner [Unai Simón](58209770.md).

The following awards were presented at the end of the tournament.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231][^cite_note-player_statistics-207]

- Golden Ball: Awarded to the best overall player of the tournament.

- Golden Boot: Awarded to the tournament's top goal scorer.

- Golden Glove: Awarded to the tournament's best goalkeeper.

- FIFA Young Player Award: Awarded to the best overall player of the tournament born on or after January 1, 2005 (aged 21 or under).

- FIFA Fair Play Trophy: Awarded to the team with the best disciplinary record that reached the knockout stage.

FIFA.com shortlisted 12 goals for users to vote on as the goal of the tournament.[^cite_note-232] The award was won by Cape Verde's [Sidny Lopes Cabral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidny_Lopes_Cabral) for his equalizing goal in extra time during the [round of 32 match against Argentina](81667041.md#Argentina_vs_Cape_Verde).[^cite_note-233]

Additionally, a "Superior Player of the Match" award was presented after each game to the player who delivered the most outstanding performance in, and had the greatest impact on, that match, as decided by a public online vote conducted by FIFA. The award was sponsored by [Michelob Ultra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelob_Ultra).[^cite_note-234] Muslim players, such as [Mohamed Salah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Salah) and [Lamine Yamal](71691936.md), received a brandless trophy, out of respect for [Islam's views on alcohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamr).[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236]

Outside of the FIFA awards, Messi was named the tournament's best player by the [International Federation of Football History and Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_of_Football_History_and_Statistics) (IFFHS), who cited his eight goals and four assists across seven appearances as the basis for the distinction.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238]

## Marketing

### Branding

A freestanding digital clock with Fifa branding placed on the sidewalk.

Countdown clock on [Paseo de la Reforma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paseo_de_la_Reforma), Mexico City in September 2025

The official logo and brand identity of the tournament was unveiled on May 17, 2023, at the [Griffith Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffith_Observatory) in [Los Angeles](18110.md), California. The logo's basic form consisted of a stacked "26" with an image of the [FIFA World Cup Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Trophy) in front of it. This was the first time that the trophy was depicted in a World Cup emblem as a photo, as opposed to a stylized representation.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-ls-242] Initial reactions to the logo were largely negative, with many saying that the design felt unfinished or uncreative compared to the stylistic logos of past FIFA World Cup tournaments.[^cite_note-ls-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] The next day, FIFA unveiled brand identities for each host city, which incorporated designs that reflected local landscapes or culture.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]



Official poster

In March and April 2025, FIFA unveiled a set of 16 posters representing each of the 2026 World Cup host cities. The posters, designed by local artists, were intended to reflect the "distinct identity and heritage" of each city.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] On March 3, 2026, the official poster for the tournament was unveiled. It was created by Carson Ting of Canada, Minerva GM of Mexico, and Hank Willis Thomas of the United States.[^cite_note-Official_posters-249]

### Broadcasting rights

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup broadcasting rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_broadcasting_rights)

On February 12, 2015, FIFA renewed the United States and Canadian broadcasting rights contracts for [Fox Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Sports_\(United_States\)) (U.S. English), [Telemundo Deportes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo_Deportes) (U.S. Spanish), and [Bell Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Media) (Canada) to cover the 2026 World Cup, without accepting any other bids. A report in *[The New York Times](30680.md)* asserted that this extension was intended as compensation for the rescheduling of the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md) to November–December rather than its traditional June–July scheduling, as it created considerable conflicts with [major professional sports leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) that were normally in their offseasons during the World Cup.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-cangloberights-252]



[Bell Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_Media) broadcast studio located at [Jack Poole Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Poole_Plaza) in Vancouver. [TSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sports_Network) was the primary broadcaster, while select matches were on [CTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTV_Television_Network) and [Crave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crave_\(streaming_service\)) in Canada.

The [International Broadcast Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Broadcast_Centre) (IBC) was located at the [Kay Bailey Hutchison Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kay_Bailey_Hutchison_Convention_Center) in [Dallas](53838.md).[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] Bell Media constructed a broadcast studio at [Jack Poole Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Poole_Plaza) in Vancouver for the tournament (mirroring an arrangement used by Fox during the [2015 FIFA Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup)), which originated [TSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sports_Network)/[CTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTV_Television_Network)'s coverage from June 11 through July 7 (the final match played in the city).[^cite_note-256]

On January 8, 2026, FIFA signed a deal to make [TikTok](56822861.md) a "preferred platform" for World Cup video content. As part of the agreement, broadcasters could stream parts of matches at a dedicated hub on the TikTok app.[^cite_note-257] FIFA then made a similar "preferred platform" deal with [YouTube](3524766.md) on March 17, allowing broadcasters to stream select matches in full on their respective YouTube channels, as well as stream the first 10 minutes of every match on the platform as "an appetizer encouraging young fans then to watch on traditional channels".[^cite_note-258]

A number of internet content creators were also involved in coverage of the tournament; in Brazil, [Casimiro Miguel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casimiro_\(streamer\))'s [CazéTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caz%C3%A9TV) renewed its digital rights to the World Cup in Brazil, streaming all matches on its YouTube channel.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] American influencer [IShowSpeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IShowSpeed) conducted streams from matches during the tournament, and later reached an agreement with Fox Sports to offer [altcasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altcast) of selected matches on [Fox One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_One) and its YouTube channel featuring his streams combined with the Fox telecasts.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263]

### Sponsorships

Main article: [2026 FIFA World Cup sponsors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_sponsors)

FIFA-level sponsorship tiers included partners, sponsors, and supporters. There were also regional supporters by geography.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265]

### Advertising



Fan Festival at the [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum) during the U.S. team's first match of the tournament.

On May 7, 2026, [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas) released *Backyard Legends: The Greatest Football Story Ever Told*, a 5-minute short film.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] On June 4, 2026, [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) released *Rip the Script*, a 6-minute short film.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269]

### FIFA fan festivals

FIFA staged [fan festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Fan_Festival) in cities across the host nations, featuring matches on giant screens and live entertainment.[^cite_note-NBCNY-270] Among the fan fest locations were [Liberty State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_State_Park) in [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey),[^cite_note-NBCNY-270] the [National World War I Museum and Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_World_War_I_Museum_and_Memorial) in [Kansas City, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri),[^cite_note-271] [Fairmount Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmount_Park) in [Philadelphia](50585.md),[^cite_note-272] [Fort York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_York) and [The Bentway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bentway) in [Toronto](64646.md),[^cite_note-273] [Centennial Olympic Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centennial_Olympic_Park) in [Atlanta](3138.md),[^cite_note-fifa-atl-fan-fest-cenn-274] [East Downtown Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Downtown_Houston),[^cite_note-275] and the [Pacific National Exhibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_National_Exhibition) in [Vancouver](32706.md), Canada.[^cite_note-276]

### Tickets

See also: [List of 2026 FIFA World Cup controversies § Ticket prices](83181783.md#Ticket_prices)

Ticket prices for the 2026 FIFA World Cup initially ranged from \$60 for group stage matches to \$6,730 for the final—an increase from the USD equivalent of \$69 to \$1,607 for the [2022 World Cup](17742072.md). However, in September 2025, FIFA confirmed it would use [dynamic pricing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamic_pricing) for tickets for the first time, following the practice used in the [2025 FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_FIFA_Club_World_Cup).[^cite_note-dp-277] Special "hospitality" seats were made available in April 2025.[^cite_note-278]

An initial draw period for non-hospitality seats occurred between September 10–19, 2025, limited to [Visa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visa_Inc.) cardholders. A second phase ran from October 27–31, 2025, and a third phase started after the final draw of teams on December 5. Sales were capped at four tickets per person per match, and no person was able to purchase more than 40 tickets for the overall tournament. FIFA's official resale platform went live on October 2, 2025.[^cite_note-dp-277][^cite_note-279]

A final "last-minute" sales phase reopened on April 22, 2026, approximately 50 days before the start of the tournament, with tickets for all 104 matches made available on a first-come, first-served basis. By that stage, more than five million tickets had been sold from an expected total exceeding six million, with additional tickets scheduled to be released in phases up to the final, subject to availability.[^cite_note-Reuters042226-280] Every city hosting the World Cup in the United States passed a law stating that ticket sales to World Cup events were exempt from state and local sales taxes.[^cite_note-284]

### Attendance

The tournament set the record for the highest cumulative attendance in World Cup history, with a total of 6,810,966 spectators.[^cite_note-285] On June 25, during the [Group E match between Ecuador and Germany](81660195.md#Ecuador_vs_Germany) at MetLife Stadium, total attendance reached 3,605,357 spectators. This surpassed the previous record of 3,587,538,[^cite_note-USATodayRecord2026-12] which had been held for 32 years by the 1994 FIFA World Cup, also hosted in the United States. The record was broken with 44 matches remaining in the tournament.[^cite_note-286] Across the first 60 matches, venues operated at an average occupancy rate of 99.7%.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] Additionally, a single-day World Cup attendance record was established on June 16, 2026, when 281,223 fans attended four group stage matches.[^cite_note-289]

### Merchandise

#### Video games

See also: [FIFA World Cup video games § 2026 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_video_games#2026_FIFA_World_Cup)

On October 2, 2025, FIFA announced the video game *[FIFA Heroes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Heroes)*, scheduled for release in 2026 on [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_\(operating_system\)), [iOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS), [Nintendo Switch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo_Switch), [PlayStation](13392949.md), and [Xbox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xbox) platforms.[^cite_note-290]

In May 2026, the *[Football Manager 26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Manager_26)* video game also announced the addition of licensed 2026 FIFA World Cup content.[^cite_note-291]

In December 2025, [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix) announced a new simulation-type game featuring the World Cup, produced by Delphi Interactive and Refactor Games.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] In May 2026, it was announced the Netflix-published game would be titled *[FIFA World Cup: Launch Edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup:_Launch_Edition)*; it was released in June 2026.[^cite_note-fs-294]

In the same May 2026 announcement, FIFA also confirmed that they would adopt a non-exclusive "Digital Football" ecosystem, with games of various genres adopting the FIFA license. Alongside the new World Cup game were *FIFA Heroes*, *FIFA Rivals, [FIFA Super Soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Roblox_games), [Football Manager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Manager), [eFootball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EFootball)* and *[Rocket League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_League),* with more games joining the ecosystem in the following months.[^cite_note-fs-294][^cite_note-295]

#### Other products



A pop-up truck selling FIFA 2026 merchandise at [Fisherman's Wharf, San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisherman's_Wharf,_San_Francisco)

[Panini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panini_Group) [sticker albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticker_album) were again announced for the 2026 World Cup. Due to the expansion of the tournament, each pack now contained seven stickers as opposed to the usual five. In the United States and Canada, a set of twelve stickers was reserved for stickers that could only be obtained from [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola) bottles, with each bottle containing a sticker printed inside the label.[^cite_note-296] In May 2026, it was announced that the 2026 World Cup would be Panini's second-to-last tournament with a sticker album, after FIFA announced that [Fanatics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fanatics,_Inc.) (via their [Topps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topps) brand) would distribute collectables for FIFA tournaments from 2031.[^cite_note-297]

In conjunction with the tournament, [the Lego Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lego_Group) released a series of officially licensed FIFA World Cup 2026-themed construction sets. The collection included models of the FIFA World Cup Trophy, the official tournament emblem, a brick-built [Adidas Trionda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Trionda) soccer ball containing a miniature stadium, and player-focused sets based on soccer players [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md), [Lionel Messi](2150841.md), [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md), and [Vinícius Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin%C3%ADcius_J%C3%BAnior). Several sets featured hidden references to the players' careers, alternative display poses, and commemorative World Cup-themed designs.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] To promote the product line, Lego organized FIFA World Cup 2026-themed activations in several markets, including a soccer festival and pop-up experience in [Singapore](27318.md). The events featured interactive soccer challenges, soccer-themed Lego builds, displays of player-inspired models, and promotional giveaways tied to the World Cup collection.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301]

## Symbols

### Mascots

Main article: [Maple, Zayu and Clutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple,_Zayu_and_Clutch)

See also: [List of FIFA World Cup official mascots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_official_mascots)

Refer to caption.

The tournament mascots: Maple the Moose, Zayu the Jaguar, and Clutch the Bald Eagle

The official mascots of the tournament were revealed on September 25, 2025, and are [Maple, Zayu and Clutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple,_Zayu_and_Clutch). Maple is a [moose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moose), Zayu is a [jaguar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaguar), and Clutch is a [bald eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bald_eagle), representing Canada, Mexico, and the United States, respectively.[^cite_note-302] They were designed to reflect the cultural heritage of their respective countries.[^cite_note-303]

### Match ball

Main article: [Adidas Trionda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Trionda)

See also: [List of FIFA World Cup official match balls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_official_match_balls)

The match ball displayed on a stand.

[Adidas Trionda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Trionda), the match ball

On May 2, 2025, reports surfaced that the match ball would be called the "[Adidas Trionda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Trionda)". The design features red, green, and blue (the three colors representing Canada, Mexico, and the United States, respectively, and also featured on the host countries' flags), as well as a white wave connecting each of the colors, hence the name using the [Spanish](26825.md) words for three (tri) and wave (onda).[^cite_note-304] The design also features the national symbols of the three host countries (a [maple leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_leaf) for Canada, a [golden eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_eagle#canadensis) for Mexico, and a [five-pointed star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five-pointed_star) for the United States) as well as gold embellishments to represent the [World Cup Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Trophy).[^cite_note-305] Later it was officially announced by Adidas on October 2, 2025.



[Adidas Trionda Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas_Trionda), used from the semifinals onwards.

On June 11, 2026, and in preparation for the 2026 FIFA World Cup, [NASA](18426568.md) conducted experiments aboard the International Space Station using soccer balls to study how mass distribution and embedded sensor technology affect ball motion and stability in microgravity. According to NASA, the research contributed to the development and evaluation of technologies used in the 2026 FIFA World Cup official match ball.[^cite_note-306] The Trionda Final was announced by Adidas on July 6, 2026, as the official match ball to be used for the semifinals, match for third place, and [final](75845879.md). It features a gold, black, and red color scheme, with the host cities' names printed on it.[^cite_note-307]

### Music

See also: [Official FIFA World Cup 2026 Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_FIFA_World_Cup_2026_Album) and [List of FIFA World Cup songs and anthems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_songs_and_anthems)

The tournament's official instrumental theme music was composed by Zachary Aaron Golden. Throughout March 2025, FIFA released remixes of the theme for each host city by local producers.[^cite_note-308]

The first single from the official soundtrack album, "[Lighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lighter_\(Jelly_Roll_and_Car%C3%ADn_Le%C3%B3n_song\))" by American [country rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_rap) performer [Jelly Roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jelly_Roll_\(singer\)) and Mexican singer [Carín León](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car%C3%ADn_Le%C3%B3n), was released on March 20, 2026; the song garnered a mixed reception from listeners and critics, with some having falsely assumed that it was meant to be the tournament's official song.[^cite_note-Conversation-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311]

The official song "[Dai Dai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dai_Dai)" by Colombian singer [Shakira](6479315.md) and Nigerian singer [Burna Boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burna_Boy) was released on May 15, 2026,[^cite_note-312] followed by the [official album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_FIFA_World_Cup_2026_Album) on June 5, 2026. The official anthem "[DNA (More Than a Game)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNA_\(More_Than_a_Game\))" by French producer [David Guetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Guetta), featuring Italian tenor [Andrea Bocelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Bocelli), South Korean-American singer [Ejae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ejae), and American rapper [Megan Thee Stallion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megan_Thee_Stallion) was released on June 10, 2026.[^cite_note-313]

Instrumental song "[Sirius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirius_\(instrumental\))" by British [rock](25423.md) band [the Alan Parsons Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Alan_Parsons_Project), known for having been played at [Chicago Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bulls) home games since 1984, was used as the theme song for the entrances of the national teams before each match during the first matchday of the group stage.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] For all subsequent matches, a special walkout remix of "Dai Dai" was used. "DNA (More Than a Game)" and "[Titanium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanium_\(David_Guetta_song\)) (David Guetta and Morten Remix)" were played after the national anthems and before the kickoff.[^cite_note-316]

## Controversies

Main article: [List of 2026 FIFA World Cup controversies](83181783.md)

The tournament was accompanied by a number of controversies relating to political, logistical, environmental, and human rights issues, particularly over the [United States' immigration and visa policies](78945778.md) affecting teams and fans, [Iran](272865.md)'s participation amid the [conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_War) during the tournament,[^cite_note-317] security concerns surrounding [drug cartel violence in Mexico](8442323.md), FIFA's use of [dynamic ticket pricing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamic_pricing#Professional_sports), the revoking of American [Folarin Balogun](60360233.md)'s one match suspension following a red card,[^cite_note-318] the application of the new rules and technology, and various other issues.

[^cite_note-162]: &#x20;When there were two or more teams tied on points, criteria a to c were applied. After these criteria were applied, they may have defined the position of some of the teams involved, but not all of them. For example, if there was a three-way tie on points, the application of the first three criteria may only have broken the tie for one of the teams, leaving the other two teams still tied. In this case, the tiebreaking procedure was resumed, from the beginning, for those teams that were still tied.
